The key distinguishing feature is the &quot;TSKF&quot; motif near the C-terminus, which is the classic catalytic signature of the transpeptidase superfamily (including PBPs and related enzymes). The single TM anchor is also consistent with many low-molecular-weight PBPs (L-MW PBPs) that have a single TM helix anchoring them to the membrane.

The question specifically mentions &quot;how the sequence&#x27;s hydrophobic-segment distribution fits the membrane topology and architecture of different enzyme families; membrane association alone is not decisive.&quot; This hints that the single TM segment with a large periplasmic catalytic domain is the key architectural feature, which matches transpeptidases (particularly low-MW PBPs).

My answer is C: Cell-wall transpeptidase activity.
